- **surprisal**: Use when reasoning about the information of one event — surprisal I(p) = −log₂(p) bits. A certain event (p=1) carries zero bits; a rare event carries many. It is the bit-cost of a specific outcome, and in erpax the bit-cost of a specific forgery: forging a thing of probability p under the wired graph costs −log₂(p) bits. Additive for independent events (I(p·q)=I(p)+I(q)), so improbabilities stack like uuid-chained dimensions; expected surprisal is Shannon entropy. ([erpax/erpax](https://github.com/erpax/erpax/tree/HEAD/src/surprisal) · ★2)

([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-environments-cold-desert-tropical-water-and-urban) · ★2)
- **survival-forest**: Comprehensive toolkit for survival analysis and time-to-event modeling in Python using scikit-survival. Use this skill when working with censored survival data, performing time-to-event analysis, fitting Cox models, Random Survival Forests, Gradient Boosting models, or Survival SVMs, evaluating survival predictions with concordance index or Brier score, handling competing risks, or implementing any survival analysis workflow with the scikit-survival library. ([jasonyandell/mk5-main](https://github.com/jasonyandell/mk5-main/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/survival-forest) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **survival-models**: Bayesian survival analysis models including exponential, Weibull, log-normal, and piecewise exponential hazard models with censoring support. ([choxos/BiostatAgent](https://github.com/choxos/BiostatAgent/tree/HEAD/plugins/bayesian-modeling/skills/survival-models) · ★11 · 2 copies)
- **survival-navigation-signalling-and-field-medicine**: Use when the situation is already going wrong: navigation and what to do once you are lost, signalling and rescue including what search and rescue actually looks for and why staying put usually wins, field medical priorities such as bleeding, airway and immobilisation, heat illness and the distinction between exhaustion and heat stroke, and animal risk placed in its real proportion. Orientation, not a substitute for medical training. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-navigation-signalling-and-field-medicine) · ★2)
- **survival-planning-kit-practice-and-myths-that-kill**: Use when preparing rather than reacting: preventive planning including trip plans and leaving word, kit that earns its weight and what most survival kits get wrong, vehicle and home preparedness, practising skills so they exist under stress, and the survival myths that actively get people killed — kept with the material they correct. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-planning-kit-practice-and-myths-that-kill) · ★2)
- **survival-reference**: Use when correcting a survival misconception, looking up a survival time, temperature, water requirement or rescue statistic, finding the sources, or needing a quick-reference picker — plus the current search-and-rescue base rates and wilderness medicine guidance. Companion to the other survival skills. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-reference) · ★2)
- **survival-shelter-water-fire-and-food**: Use for the core needs in their actual order of urgency: thermoregulation and shelter including how fast hypothermia develops and what insulation and wind actually do, water with the real timelines, treatment options and the risks of untreated sources, fire and reliable ignition, and food — why it comes last and how long you genuinely have without it. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-shelter-water-fire-and-food) · ★2)
- **survival-what-kills-psychology-and-priorities**: Use first in any survival question, because it reorders everything else: what actually kills people in the outdoors and how far that is from the popular picture, survival psychology including panic, denial and the decisions that get people killed, and the priority ordering that follows from the physiology rather than from intuition. Includes the router for the whole survival reference. ([the-vibey-project/vibey](https://github.com/the-vibey-project/vibey/tree/HEAD/src/vibey_tools/skills/plugins/survival-skills/skills/survival-what-kills-psychology-and-priorities) · ★2)
